Um... well, I know everyone gets overwhelmed sometimes, and I really don't want to sound dramatic or anything. I mean, I know I have a lot to be thankful for. But... I guess things have just felt so heavy lately.

Um... well, I really try to stay on top of my work, you know? I don't want to let my team down or anything. But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, things have been taking me a little longer lately. 

It's just silly things, really. Like, I'll look at a basic drainage calculation, or a spreadsheet that I've literally used for years, and my mind just kind of... blanks. I just sit there rereading the same numbers over and over, and I can't seem to trust myself to make a decision on it. It's so frustrating because I know how to do it. I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job, so I end up double and triple-checking everything, which slows me down. 

I'm sure it's just because I'm a little tired, right? I really hope it's not affecting my performance too much. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your questions right.

Um... I mean, I know I shouldn't be so hard on myself. Everyone makes mistakes, right? And I really try to be positive and do my best for everyone. But... lately, I just... I can't seem to stop feeling like I'm just... messing everything up. 

I keep telling myself that I'm a terrible engineer, and honestly, a failed wife, too. It just feels like I'm this huge burden to the people around me. I know it's probably just my mind playing tricks on me, but I keep replaying every little missed calculation at work, or every tense email I've sent... and it just feels like absolute proof that I ruin things. 

I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself, though. I really do try to push past it. It's just hard to ignore sometimes. Is it normal to get stuck on things like that when you're stressed out? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the full picture.

I just can't seem to shake this really heavy, hopeless feeling. \n\nIt kind of feels like everything I tried to build after my divorce has just... collapsed. And I'm just sort of sitting under it, if that makes sense? I know that's probably a lot to drop on you in the first five minutes, I'm sorry. Am I explaining this okay?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, hopeless feeling?\n\nPatient: Um... I'd say it's been about a month? Give or take. It really started to, um, hang over me right around the time I was dealing with all the financial... well, the financial mess after the divorce. I mean, I know everyone gets stressed about money and breakups, so I really try not to complain too much, and I'm sure it's probably a normal reaction... but it just hasn't lifted since then. Is that a normal timeframe for something like this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I still try to keep up with things, you know? I don't want to just sit around and do nothing. But... well, I used to love reading, and lately I just can't seem to focus on a book. Just... maybe simpler things, or whatever is easy to grab. \n\nI haven't noticed any major changes on the scale or anything like that. I try to make sure I'm still taking care of myself in that way, so I don't think there's anything to worry about there. Is that what you were looking for? I hope that's helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Um... well, maybe a little bit? Does that sound like the kind of trouble sleeping you mean, or is that just normal stress?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I know everyone gets overwhelmed sometimes, and I really don't want to sound dramatic or anything. I hope that's helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced significant impairment in social, occupational, or other important areas of functioning nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Um... well... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, no, not two months. It hasn't been that long, I promise. I've really been trying not to let it drag on, because I know I need to get back to normal.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, maybe a little bit? I mean, I try really hard to stay on top of things, especially at work. I don't want anyone to think I'm slacking off or that I can't do my job. But... lately, I guess it has been a bit harder to just... make a call on things. 

Like, I'll be looking at a basic drainage calculation, or a spreadsheet I've used for years as a civil engineer, and my mind just sort of... blanks? I end up sitting there, rereading the same numbers over and over, and I just can't seem to trust myself to make the right decision. I know I should be able to do it easily, and I really am trying to push through it. I just... I don't want to make a mistake and cause problems for anyone else. Does that make sense? I'm hoping it's just a temporary thing and I'll be back to my normal self soon.
